Um... I don't know if I'd use the word "afraid," exactly. I just really like feeling disciplined, if that makes sense?

Well... I mean, nobody really *likes* gaining weight, right? It's just human nature to want to look your best, especially in my line of work where you're always front-facing with clients. 

But, um... I guess I do get a little... stressed, maybe? Like, if I have a big client dinner, or say I'm away for a weekend down in Miami, and I come back and the scale is even a pound higher... I do get this sort of... panic. It's just this feeling like I'm losing control of things. And then I immediately start planning out exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. 

I know that probably sounds a little intense, but it's just because I like to stay on top of things. It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.

Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way.

I know some people struggle with that kind of thing, but I've never... no. \n\nI mean, throwing up just seems so—well, it's not something I'd ever want to do. I try to manage things just by being careful with my portions in the first place, so I don't ever feel like I need to resort to something like that. That's a much healthier way to go about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't call it excessive. I do work out, of course. I think it's important to stay active, especially in sales where you're sitting in meetings or driving around so much. \n\nI might hit the gym for a bit, or maybe go for a run, just to stay sharp. Sometimes if I had a heavier dinner the night before, I might stay on the treadmill a little longer the next morning, just to balance things out. But it's nothing crazy. Just normal maintenance, you know? It's good to stay on top of your fitness, right? I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know my family and my coworkers have been making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, saying I look too thin or whatever. But when I look in the mirror, I just don't see it as this huge issue they're making it out to be. 

I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly fine, right? I still get up every morning, I still go for my jog, I'm out there working my territory every day, and I'm still hitting all my sales numbers. If I was really as underweight as they say, or if it was some kind of medical emergency, I wouldn't be able to do all that, would I? 

I feel like as long as I have the energy to do my job and stay active, I must be doing okay. That's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you think? I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.

I do work out, of course. I think it's important to stay active, especially in sales where you're sitting in meetings or driving around so much. \n\nI might hit the gym for a bit, or maybe go for a run, just to stay sharp. Sometimes if I had a heavier dinner the night before, I might stay on the treadmill a little longer the next morning, just to balance things out. But it's nothing crazy. Just normal maintenance, you know? It's good to stay on top of your fitness, right? I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, nobody really *likes* gaining weight, right? It's just human nature to want to look your best, especially in my line of work where you're always front-facing with clients. \n\nBut, um... I guess I do get a little... stressed, maybe? Like, if I have a big client dinner, or say I'm away for a weekend down in Miami, and I come back and the scale is even a pound higher... I do get this sort of... panic. It's just this feeling like I'm losing control of things. And then I immediately start planning out exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. \n\nI know that probably sounds a little intense, but it's just because I like to stay on top of things. It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way. \n\nLike, if I'm getting ready in the morning, checking the mirror before a big presentation or, you know, maybe going out on a date... I tend to just focus right in on my stomach or my face. And if I look even a little bit soft or puffy, it just... it bothers me. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right? I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. I always make sure to keep it professional and under control. That's the best way to handle those situations, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around bingeing or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. No, I would never do that. I know some people struggle with that kind of thing, but I've never... no. \n\nI mean, throwing up just seems so—well, it's not something I'd ever want to do. I do work out, of course. I think it's important to stay active, especially in sales where you're sitting in meetings or driving around so much. \n\nI might hit the gym for a bit, or maybe go for a run, just to stay sharp. Sometimes if I had a heavier dinner the night before, I might stay on the treadmill a little longer the next morning, just to balance things out. But it's nothing crazy. Just normal maintenance, you know? It's good to stay on top of your fitness, right? I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. Well... um... I wouldn't say it completely stops me from doing things. I mean, I still manage my sales route just fine, and I'm out there every day talking to people. So I'm definitely not, like, hiding away or anything. 

But... I guess, maybe... I have been turning down a few more invitations lately. Just things like client dinners or, you know, going on dates. It's just... it's a lot easier if I can control the menu, or at least know exactly what's going to be served. Restaurants can just be so unpredictable with how they prepare things, and I really prefer to stick to my routine. 

So, I suppose I have been keeping to myself a little bit more outside of work. But I'm still getting my job done, and that's the most important thing, right? It's just a temporary thing while I stay focused on my goals. That's not too unusual for someone driven in their career, is it?

I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current weight is lower than what most people would consider normal for someone of your height and build?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess if you look at a chart or something, maybe it's lower than what some people think is normal. But like I was saying before, I know everyone is making such a big deal about my weight, but... I'm still jogging every morning, I'm still out there working my territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. \n\nSo, to me, it just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency, you know? If my weight was really that dangerously low, I wouldn't have the stamina to keep up with all of that. I'm just trying to stay fit and keep my edge. That makes sense, right? I'm not trying to cause any trouble or worry anyone.\n\nDoctor: Does your concern about gaining weight affect your ability to enjoy social events or spend time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Well... um... I wouldn't say it completely stops me from doing things. I mean, I still manage my sales route just fine, and I'm out there every day talking to people. So I'm definitely not, like, hiding away or anything. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe... I have been turning down a few more invitations lately. Just things like client dinners or, you know, going on dates. It's just... it's a lot easier if I can control the menu, or at least know exactly what's going to be served. Restaurants can just be so unpredictable with how they prepare things, and I really prefer to stick to my routine. \n\nSo, I suppose I have been keeping to myself a little bit more outside of work. But I'm still getting my job done, and that's the most important thing, right? It's just a temporary thing while I stay focused on my goals. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... um, I guess I do have a list of things I try to avoid. It's just about being disciplined, really. Being in sales, I'm on the road a lot, and it's so easy to just grab whatever's quick and unhealthy. So I just try to be careful. That's a good habit to have, right? 

But... I mean, I suppose I do get a little... anxious about it sometimes. Like, if I have to go to a client dinner, or if I'm out for a weekend in Miami with some buddies, and I step on the scale the next day... if it's even just a pound higher, I... I get this awful panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control of everything. 

So then I just start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day, you know? Just to get things back on track. It's not like I'm starving myself, I just want to keep everything balanced. That's a pretty normal way to handle a slip-up, don't you think? I'm just trying to stay on top of things.

Like, if I step on the scale after a big client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami, and it's even a pound higher... I get this sort of sudden panic. It just feels like I'm losing control. \n\nSo I usually start planning right away how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. I hope that doesn't sound too extreme? I just try to be proactive so things don't get out of hand. That's a pretty normal way to manage things, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't really do that. I mean, I try to be really careful about what I eat, so I don't really let myself get to a point where I'm just... eating a ton all at once. \n\nSometimes I might have a little more than I planned if I'm out with a client, like maybe an extra appetizer or something, but I wouldn't call it excessive. I always make sure to keep it professional and under control. That's the best way to handle those situations, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around bingeing or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. No, I would never do that. That's a much healthier way to go about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't call it excessive. I do work out, of course. I think it's important to stay active, especially in sales where you're sitting in meetings or driving around so much. \n\nI might hit the gym for a bit, or maybe go for a run, just to stay sharp. Sometimes if I had a heavier dinner the night before, I might stay on the treadmill a little longer the next morning, just to balance things out. But it's nothing crazy. Just normal maintenance, you know? It's good to stay on top of your fitness, right? I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, nobody really *likes* gaining weight, right? It's just human nature to want to look your best, especially in my line of work where you're always front-facing with clients. \n\nBut, um... I guess I do get a little... stressed, maybe? Like, if I have a big client dinner, or say I'm away for a weekend down in Miami, and I come back and the scale is even a pound higher... I do get this sort of... panic. It's just this feeling like I'm losing control of things. And then I immediately start planning out exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. \n\nI know that probably sounds a little intense, but it's just because I like to stay on top of things. It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way. \n\nLike, if I'm getting ready in the morning, checking the mirror before a big presentation or, you know, maybe going out on a date... I tend to just focus right in on my stomach or my face. And if I look even a little bit soft or puffy, it just... it bothers me. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right? I'm sure you understand how important it is to make a good impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you're trying to achieve?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say there's a specific number I'm chasing, exactly. I don't really have some sort of chart or, you know, a rigid goal in mind. \n\nI think it's more just about... feeling right? Like I mentioned, it's just about looking sharp in my suits and feeling like I have that edge when I walk into a room. I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. I guess I'm just trying to maintain a look that says I'm successful and in control. \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I'm just trying to be the best I can be for my career. I'm not trying to do anything extreme, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see yourself as being underweight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. But when I look in the mirror, I just don't see it as this huge issue they're making it out to be. \n\nI mean, I'm still functioning perfectly fine, right? I still get up every morning, I still go for my jog, I'm out there working my territory every day, and I'm still hitting all my sales numbers. That's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you think? I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current weight is lower than what most people would consider normal for someone of your height and build?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess if you look at a chart or something, maybe it's lower than what some people think is normal. But like I was saying before, I know everyone is making such a big deal about my weight, but... I'm still jogging every morning, I'm still out there working my territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. \n\nSo, to me, it just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency, you know? I do get this sort of... panic. It's just this feeling like I'm losing control of things. And then I immediately start planning out exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. \n\nI know that probably sounds a little intense, but it's just because I like to stay on top of things. It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way. \n\nLike, if I'm getting ready in the morning, checking the mirror before a big presentation or, you know, maybe going out on a date... I tend to just focus right in on my stomach or my face. And if I look even a little bit soft or puffy, it just... it bothers me. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right? I'm sure you understand how important it is to make a good impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you're trying to achieve?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say there's a specific number I'm chasing, exactly. I don't really have some sort of chart or, you know, a rigid goal in mind. \n\nI think it's more just about... feeling right? Like I mentioned, it's just about looking sharp in my suits and feeling like I have that edge when I walk into a room. I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. I guess I'm just trying to maintain a look that says I'm successful and in control. \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I'm just trying to be the best I can be for my career. I'm not trying to do anything extreme, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see yourself as being underweight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know my family and my coworkers have been making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, saying I look too thin or whatever. But when I look in the mirror, I just don't see it as this huge issue they're making it out to be. \n\nI mean, I'm still functioning perfectly fine, right? I still get up every morning, I still go for my jog, I'm out there working my territory every day, and I'm still hitting all my sales numbers. If I was really as underweight as they say, or if it was some kind of medical emergency, I wouldn't be able to do all that, would I? \n\nI feel like as long as I have the energy to do my job and stay active, I must be doing okay. That's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you think? I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current weight is lower than what most people would consider normal for someone of your height and build?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess if you look at a chart or something, maybe it's lower than what some people think is normal. But like I was saying before, I know everyone is making such a big deal about my weight, but... I'm still jogging every morning, I'm still out there working my territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. \n\nSo, to me, it just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency, you know? If my weight was really that dangerously low, I wouldn't have the stamina to keep up with all of that. I'm just trying to stay fit and keep my edge. That makes sense, right? I'm not trying to cause any trouble or worry anyone.\n\nDoctor: Does your concern about gaining weight affect your ability to enjoy social events or spend time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Well... um... I wouldn't say it completely stops me from doing things. I mean, I still manage my sales route just fine, and I'm out there every day talking to people. So I'm definitely not, like, hiding away or anything. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe... I have been turning down a few more invitations lately. Just things like client dinners or, you know, going on dates. It's just... it's a lot easier if I can control the menu, or at least know exactly what's going to be served. Restaurants can just be so unpredictable with how they prepare things, and I really prefer to stick to my routine. \n\nSo, I suppose I have been keeping to myself a little bit more outside of work. But I'm still getting my job done, and that's the most important thing, right? It's just a temporary thing while I stay focused on my goals. That's a good habit to have, right? \n\nBut... I mean, I suppose I do get a little... anxious about it sometimes. I'm just trying to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Are there any relationships in your life that have been affected by your concerns about weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I make sure of that. \n\nAnd as for my friends or family... well, I guess my buddies might give me a hard time sometimes if I don't want to grab beers or eat bar food, but it's just guys joking around, you know? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... um, I don't know if I'd say it affects how I feel about myself as a *person*, exactly. I mean, I know I'm a good guy, a hard worker... 

But, I guess... well, appearance is really important in sales, right? You have to look sharp. So, sometimes... like before a big client meeting or if I'm going out on a date, I'll look in the mirror and... I don't know, I just kind of zero in on my stomach or my face. And if I feel like I look a little... soft, I guess... it just really throws me off. 

I start worrying that if I look out of shape, people are going to think I'm sloppy, or... or weak, maybe? Like I don't have the discipline to take care of myself. And in my line of work, you really want to project confidence and control. So I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about when you're meeting new people, isn't it? I just want to make a good impression.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I hope I'm not taking up a spot from someone who really needs it. \n\nUm... honestly, I'm really only here because HR at my company and my sister keep saying I've gotten a bit too thin. I hate causing a fuss or making anyone worry, so I figured I should just come in to put their minds at ease. Is that okay? \n\nFrom my perspective, I just see that my suits are fitting a lot sharper, which is really great for sales. I guess my weight has kept dropping since my divorce, but... well, that's a pretty normal reaction to stress, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by being here.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word \"afraid,\" exactly. I just really like feeling disciplined, if that makes sense? \n\nBut, I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information here—I do get a little... stressed about it. Like, if I step on the scale after a big client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami, and it's even a pound higher... I get this sort of sudden panic. It just feels like I'm losing control. \n\nSo I usually start planning right away how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. I hope that doesn't sound too extreme? I just try to be proactive so things don't get out of hand. That's a pretty normal way to manage things, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't really do that. I mean, I try to be really careful about what I eat, so I don't really let myself get to a point where I'm just... eating a ton all at once. \n\nSometimes I might have a little more than I planned if I'm out with a client, like maybe an extra appetizer or something, but I wouldn't call it excessive. I always make sure to keep it professional and under control. That's the best way to handle those situations, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around bingeing or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. No, I would never do that. I know some people struggle with that kind of thing, but I've never... no. \n\nI mean, throwing up just seems so—well, it's not something I'd ever want to do. I try to manage things just by being careful with my portions in the first place, so I don't ever feel like I need to resort to something like that. That's a much healthier way to go about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't call it excessive. I do work out, of course. I think it's important to stay active, especially in sales where you're sitting in meetings or driving around so much. \n\nI might hit the gym for a bit, or maybe go for a run, just to stay sharp. Sometimes if I had a heavier dinner the night before, I might stay on the treadmill a little longer the next morning, just to balance things out. But it's nothing crazy. Just normal maintenance, you know? It's good to stay on top of your fitness, right? I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, nobody really *likes* gaining weight, right? It's just human nature to want to look your best, especially in my line of work where you're always front-facing with clients. \n\nBut, um... I guess I do get a little... stressed, maybe? Like, if I have a big client dinner, or say I'm away for a weekend down in Miami, and I come back and the scale is even a pound higher... I do get this sort of... panic. It's just this feeling like I'm losing control of things. And then I immediately start planning out exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. \n\nI know that probably sounds a little intense, but it's just because I like to stay on top of things. It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way. \n\nLike, if I'm getting ready in the morning, checking the mirror before a big presentation or, you know, maybe going out on a date... I tend to just focus right in on my stomach or my face. And if I look even a little bit soft or puffy, it just... it bothers me. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right? I'm sure you understand how important it is to make a good impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you're trying to achieve?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say there's a specific number I'm chasing, exactly. I don't really have some sort of chart or, you know, a rigid goal in mind. \n\nI think it's more just about... feeling right? Like I mentioned, it's just about looking sharp in my suits and feeling like I have that edge when I walk into a room. I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. I guess I'm just trying to maintain a look that says I'm successful and in control. \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I'm just trying to be the best I can be for my career. I'm not trying to do anything extreme, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see yourself as being underweight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know my family and my coworkers have been making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, saying I look too thin or whatever. But when I look in the mirror, I just don't see it as this huge issue they're making it out to be. \n\nI mean, I'm still functioning perfectly fine, right? I still get up every morning, I still go for my jog, I'm out there working my territory every day, and I'm still hitting all my sales numbers. If I was really as underweight as they say, or if it was some kind of medical emergency, I wouldn't be able to do all that, would I? \n\nI feel like as long as I have the energy to do my job and stay active, I must be doing okay. That's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you think? I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current weight is lower than what most people would consider normal for someone of your height and build?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess if you look at a chart or something, maybe it's lower than what some people think is normal. But like I was saying before, I know everyone is making such a big deal about my weight, but... I'm still jogging every morning, I'm still out there working my territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. \n\nSo, to me, it just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency, you know? If my weight was really that dangerously low, I wouldn't have the stamina to keep up with all of that. I'm just trying to stay fit and keep my edge. That makes sense, right? I'm not trying to cause any trouble or worry anyone.\n\nDoctor: Does your concern about gaining weight affect your ability to enjoy social events or spend time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Well... um... I wouldn't say it completely stops me from doing things. I mean, I still manage my sales route just fine, and I'm out there every day talking to people. So I'm definitely not, like, hiding away or anything. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe... I have been turning down a few more invitations lately. Just things like client dinners or, you know, going on dates. It's just... it's a lot easier if I can control the menu, or at least know exactly what's going to be served. Restaurants can just be so unpredictable with how they prepare things, and I really prefer to stick to my routine. \n\nSo, I suppose I have been keeping to myself a little bit more outside of work. But I'm still getting my job done, and that's the most important thing, right? It's just a temporary thing while I stay focused on my goals. That's not too unusual for someone driven in their career, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I guess I do have a list of things I try to avoid. It's just about being disciplined, really. Being in sales, I'm on the road a lot, and it's so easy to just grab whatever's quick and unhealthy. So I just try to be careful. That's a good habit to have, right? \n\nBut... I mean, I suppose I do get a little... anxious about it sometimes. Like, if I have to go to a client dinner, or if I'm out for a weekend in Miami with some buddies, and I step on the scale the next day... if it's even just a pound higher, I... I get this awful panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control of everything. \n\nSo then I just start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day, you know? Just to get things back on track. It's not like I'm starving myself, I just want to keep everything balanced. That's a pretty normal way to handle a slip-up, don't you think? I'm just trying to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Are there any relationships in your life that have been affected by your concerns about weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I mean, like I mentioned, I might pass on a dinner here and there, but my professional relationships are totally fine. I make sure of that. \n\nAnd as for my friends or family... well, I guess my buddies might give me a hard time sometimes if I don't want to grab beers or eat bar food, but it's just guys joking around, you know? It hasn't actually caused any real problems. They know I'm just really dedicated to my fitness and my career right now. \n\nI don't think anyone is actually upset with me or anything like that. I really try to be a good friend and keep in touch with everyone, even if I'm not always going out to eat with them. That's the main thing, right? As long as I'm still doing my job and checking in on people, it shouldn't be an issue, should it?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about weight affected your work performance or daily routines outside of social events?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say so at all. Actually, my sales numbers have been really strong lately. I'm really careful to make sure my... my habits don't ever interfere with my job. \n\nI mean, I do have a pretty strict daily routine. I usually wake up pretty early to get my workout in before I hit the road for my accounts, and I always pack my own meals for the day so I'm not caught off guard while I'm driving around. But... that's just being prepared, right? It actually makes me more efficient because I'm not wasting time figuring out where to stop for lunch. \n\nSo, no, it hasn't hurt my performance or anything like that. If anything, I think being this organized just shows I'm disciplined, don't you think? I always make sure I'm doing a good job and meeting my quotas.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is affecting how you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I don't know if I'd say it affects how I feel about myself as a *person*, exactly. I just want to make a good impression.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um... wow, that's a really good question. I guess I haven't really thought about it like that before. I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. 

I've been in sales for a while now, so it's kind of hard to separate myself from that environment, you know? But... I mean, I don't think I'm *that* strict with it, really. Even if I had a desk job where I was just behind a computer all day and nobody saw me, I'd still want to take care of myself. 

It's really just about being healthy and... and staying active. I think anyone would want to keep themselves in good shape regardless of what they do for a living, right? It just feels better when you eat clean and get your workouts in. So I'd probably still do the same things, just because I want to be healthy. That's a pretty normal way to look at it, don't you think? I just try to do what I think is best for my overall health.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I hope I'm not taking up a spot from someone who really needs it. \n\nUm... honestly, I'm really only here because HR at my company and my sister keep saying I've gotten a bit too thin. Is that okay? \n\nFrom my perspective, I just see that my suits are fitting a lot sharper, which is really great for sales. Like, if I step on the scale after a big client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami, and it's even a pound higher... I get this sort of sudden panic. It just feels like I'm losing control. \n\nSo I usually start planning right away how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. I hope that doesn't sound too extreme? I just try to be proactive so things don't get out of hand. I just try to be disciplined about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I'm doing fine. I mean, I know everyone around me is making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, and I know they mean well, but... I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I were really at an unhealthy weight, I wouldn't have the energy to keep up with all of that, right? It just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency to me. As long as I'm functioning well and doing my job, that means my body is handling things perfectly well, don't you think? I just want to assure you that I'm keeping on top of my responsibilities and taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be seriously upset if you gained a small amount of weight, like a few pounds?\n\nPatient: Well... It's just about being disciplined, you know? I'm sure a lot of people feel that way when they see the scale go up, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know other people say I do, but when I look at myself, I just... I don't see it that way. \n\nLike, if I'm getting ready in the morning, checking the mirror before a big presentation or, you know, maybe going out on a date... I tend to just focus right in on my stomach or my face. And if I look even a little bit soft or puffy, it just... it bothers me. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right? I'm sure you understand how important it is to make a good impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you're trying to achieve?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say there's a specific number I'm chasing, exactly. I don't really have some sort of chart or, you know, a rigid goal in mind. \n\nI think it's more just about... feeling right? Like I mentioned, it's just about looking sharp in my suits and feeling like I have that edge when I walk into a room. I just want to feel like I'm presenting the best, most disciplined version of myself. I guess I'm just trying to maintain a look that says I'm successful and in control. \n\nThat makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I'm just trying to be the best I can be for my career. I'm not trying to do anything extreme, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see yourself as being underweight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I mean, I know my family and my coworkers have been making a pretty big deal about my weight lately, saying I look too thin or whatever. But when I look in the mirror, I just don't see it as this huge issue they're making it out to be. \n\nI mean, I'm still functioning perfectly fine, right? I still get up every morning, I still go for my jog, I'm out there working my territory every day, and I'm still hitting all my sales numbers. If I was really as underweight as they say, or if it was some kind of medical emergency, I wouldn't be able to do all that, would I? \n\nI feel like as long as I have the energy to do my job and stay active, I must be doing okay. That's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you think? I just don't want anyone to worry unnecessarily when I'm still handling everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current weight is lower than what most people would consider normal for someone of your height and build?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess if you look at a chart or something, maybe it's lower than what some people think is normal. But like I was saying before, I know everyone is making such a big deal about my weight, but... I'm still jogging every morning, I'm still out there working my territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. \n\nSo, to me, it just doesn't feel like some kind of medical emergency, you know? If my weight was really that dangerously low, I wouldn't have the stamina to keep up with all of that. I'm just trying to stay fit and keep my edge. That makes sense, right? I'm not trying to cause any trouble or worry anyone.\n\nDoctor: Does your concern about gaining weight affect your ability to enjoy social events or spend time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Well... um... I wouldn't say it completely stops me from doing things. I mean, I still manage my sales route just fine, and I'm out there every day talking to people. So I'm definitely not, like, hiding away or anything. \n\nBut... I guess, maybe... I have been turning down a few more invitations lately. Just things like client dinners or, you know, going on dates. It's just... it's a lot easier if I can control the menu, or at least know exactly what's going to be served. Restaurants can just be so unpredictable with how they prepare things, and I really prefer to stick to my routine. \n\nSo, I suppose I have been keeping to myself a little bit more outside of work. But I'm still getting my job done, and that's the most important thing, right? It's just a temporary thing while I stay focused on my goals. That's not too unusual for someone driven in their career, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I guess I do have a list of things I try to avoid. It's just about being disciplined, really. Being in sales, I'm on the road a lot, and it's so easy to just grab whatever's quick and unhealthy. So I just try to be careful. That's a good habit to have, right? \n\nBut... I mean, I suppose I do get a little... anxious about it sometimes. Like, if I have to go to a client dinner, or if I'm out for a weekend in Miami with some buddies, and I step on the scale the next day... if it's even just a pound higher, I... I get this awful panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control of everything. \n\nSo then I just start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day, you know? Just to get things back on track. It's not like I'm starving myself, I just want to keep everything balanced. That's a pretty normal way to handle a slip-up, don't you think? I'm just trying to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Are there any relationships in your life that have been affected by your concerns about weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I mean, like I mentioned, I might pass on a dinner here and there, but my professional relationships are totally fine. I make sure of that. \n\nAnd as for my friends or family... well, I guess my buddies might give me a hard time sometimes if I don't want to grab beers or eat bar food, but it's just guys joking around, you know? It hasn't actually caused any real problems. They know I'm just really dedicated to my fitness and my career right now. \n\nI don't think anyone is actually upset with me or anything like that. I really try to be a good friend and keep in touch with everyone, even if I'm not always going out to eat with them. That's the main thing, right? As long as I'm still doing my job and checking in on people, it shouldn't be an issue, should it?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about weight affected your work performance or daily routines outside of social events?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say so at all. Actually, my sales numbers have been really strong lately. I'm really careful to make sure my... my habits don't ever interfere with my job. \n\nI mean, I do have a pretty strict daily routine. I usually wake up pretty early to get my workout in before I hit the road for my accounts, and I always pack my own meals for the day so I'm not caught off guard while I'm driving around. But... that's just being prepared, right? It actually makes me more efficient because I'm not wasting time figuring out where to stop for lunch. \n\nSo, no, it hasn't hurt my performance or anything like that. If anything, I think being this organized just shows I'm disciplined, don't you think? I always make sure I'm doing a good job and meeting my quotas.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is affecting how you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I don't know if I'd say it affects how I feel about myself as a *person*, exactly. I mean, I know I'm a good guy, a hard worker... \n\nBut, I guess... well, appearance is really important in sales, right? You have to look sharp. So, sometimes... like before a big client meeting or if I'm going out on a date, I'll look in the mirror and... I don't know, I just kind of zero in on my stomach or my face. And if I feel like I look a little... soft, I guess... it just really throws me off. \n\nI start worrying that if I look out of shape, people are going to think I'm sloppy, or... or weak, maybe? Like I don't have the discipline to take care of myself. And in my line of work, you really want to project confidence and control. So I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. That's a pretty normal thing to worry about when you're meeting new people, isn't it? I just want to make a good impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your weight is affecting your overall self-worth or how you feel about yourself outside of your professional life?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really wouldn't say so. I mean, sure, like I said, I want to look good if I'm going out on a date or something, but... it doesn't change who I am underneath, you know? I still know I'm a good guy, a loyal friend, all of that. \n\nI don't let my... my fitness goals dictate my entire value as a human being or anything. I try to keep a really positive mindset about it all. I mean, everyone wants to feel attractive and put-together in their personal life, right? It's just normal to care about your appearance. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to control your weight as strictly if you didn't have the pressure of looking professional for your sales job?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a really good question. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean... I don't know if I'd call it a fear, exactly. That sounds a little extreme, right? But... I guess I do get a bit stressed out about it. 

Like, if the scale is even a pound higher after a big client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami or something, I do get this... this panic, I guess. It just feels like I'm losing control. So I'll immediately start planning out how to cut back the next day to fix it and get it back down. 

But I feel like I just like to stay on top of it, you know? I think it's pretty normal to want to course-correct if you see the numbers creeping up. That's really just being disciplined with your body, don't you think?

I honestly don't see the problem. \n\nI'm still jogging, I'm out there working my territory every single day, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. I just want to make sure I'm staying on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well... um, no, not really. I start worrying that clients are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy, or that I lack discipline, you know? Like I'm weak. In sales, presentation is everything. You have to look sharp and put-together. \n\nI just want to look professional and capable. That's a normal thing to care about, right?
